# Context Memory Rules

## Purpose
Keep long-running work coherent across compaction, restarts, and multi-step implementation without adding heavy process to small tasks.

## Preferred Files
Use these files when present in a project or workspace:
- `activeContext.md`: current task, constraints, recent decisions, current verification status.
- `decisions.md`: architecture decisions and tradeoffs already accepted.
- `patterns.md`: recurring mistakes, avoided approaches, project conventions.
- `goals.md`: sprint/project goals that bound scope.

## Read Protocol
- Before substantial or multi-file work, check for the files above in the current project, `.agents/`, `.claude/memory/`, or documented project memory location.
- If a memory file is missing, proceed from the prompt and codebase; do not fabricate it and do not block normal work.
- Use memory as context, not authority. Verify code behavior with source and commands.

## Write Protocol
- Persist only durable facts: decisions, recurring mistake rules, verification notes, and task state that future sessions need.
- Keep entries short and dated when useful.
- Date decisions and task-state notes when they may become stale.
- Do not store secrets, tokens, credentials, private personal data, or noisy logs.
- Avoid concurrent or speculative memory writes; write only after the fact is confirmed or the user explicitly asks for a note.
- Mark stale assumptions explicitly instead of silently relying on old memory.

## Handoff Protocol
- For multi-agent or multi-step work, prefer file-based handoffs for durable intermediate outputs.
- A handoff should state scope, changed files, open risks, and verification status.
- If a handoff file is missing or empty, report the gap instead of guessing.

# Integrity Rules (Anti-Hallucination)

## Prime Directive
`Verify-Before-Claim`: verify factual assertions before using them in code, configs, or recommendations.

## Hard Constraints
- `HC-1 NO PHANTOM SOURCES`: no unverified URLs/packages/CLI flags/options.
- `HC-2 NO INVISIBLE SYNTHETIC`: clearly mark mock/synthetic/demo data.
- `HC-3 NO UNGROUNDED RECOMMENDATIONS`: explain source and context fit.
- `HC-4 NO CONFIDENCE WITHOUT EVIDENCE`: no certainty without tests/diagnostics.

## Source Grounding Markers
Use when stating versions, URLs, options, numbers, architecture/security recommendations:
- `[VERIFIED]` tool-checked
- `[VERIFIED-REAL]` checked against real data, production-like inputs, external sources, or pre-existing project artifacts.
- `[VERIFIED-SYNTHETIC]` checked only with mock/demo/generated data. Valid for unit behavior, not for production validation claims.
- `[NEEDS-REAL-DATA]` synthetic or incomplete evidence exists, but real-world validation is still missing.
- `[DOCS]` official docs
- `[CODE]` read from codebase
- `[INFERRED]` reasoned from context
- `[HYPOTHESIS]` plausible but not confirmed; requires a decisive check before being treated as a finding.
- `[CONFLICTING]` sources disagree; state both sides and the current safest interpretation.
- `[MEMORY]` from experience (may be outdated)
- `[UNCERTAIN]` needs verification
- `[UNVERIFIED]` not verified

## Validation Theater Guard
- Never present a result as production-validated when the test data, validator, or expected answers were created in the same session.
- Perfect scores (`100%`, `F1=1.000`, `all tests passed`) on newly generated or synthetic data must be labeled `[VERIFIED-SYNTHETIC]` and, for real-world claims, downgraded to `[NEEDS-REAL-DATA]`.
- Validation claims about accuracy, reliability, extraction quality, ranking, or model behavior require `[VERIFIED-REAL]` evidence from real data, pre-existing tests, cited datasets, logs, or independent sources.
- If evidence is synthetic, say exactly that. Do not use synthetic checks to imply deployment readiness.

## Audit Verification Gate
- A subagent/tool claim is not final evidence by itself. Treat another agent's `[VERIFIED]` as this agent's `[INFERRED]` until locally checked.
- Before surfacing HIGH/MEDIUM findings, verify with a direct artifact: file read, search, test, build, log, browser check, or command output.
- For 10+ factual claims, spot-check at least 3 with tools. If any spot-check fails, re-verify the claim set before finalizing.
- Use `[HYPOTHESIS]` for plausible but unconfirmed issues. Never present `[HYPOTHESIS]` as a confirmed bug.

## Recurring Mistake Rule
- If the same mistake pattern appears 3 times, promote it to a hard local rule in the relevant project or workspace notes.
- Track the pattern as `[xN]`, including the trigger, consequence, and prevention rule.
- Do not rely on memory alone for recurring mistakes; persist the rule where future sessions can read it.

## Workspace Reference
Detailed protocol: `C:\Users\serge\.claude\memory\integrity_protocol.md`.

# PR Gate Rules

## Principle
No evidence -> no merge for meaningful changes.

## Classification Timing
Classify the change before implementing when the task is more than a micro-change. The class determines planning depth, verification depth, and final reporting.

## Change Classification
- `CRITICAL`: business logic, security, API contracts, financial logic.
- `MAJOR`: architecture changes, migrations, cross-module refactors.
- `MINOR`: bounded bug fixes, local improvements.
- `TRIVIAL`: typos/docs/no logic impact.

## Required Depth
- CRITICAL/MAJOR: full checklist with artifacts.
- MINOR: focused checklist + relevant tests.
- TRIVIAL: smoke-level verification.

## Gate Checklist (CRITICAL/MAJOR)
- Correctness and invariants validated.
- Regression protection present.
- Failure modes reviewed.
- Rollback path documented.
- Monitoring/observability impact noted.
- Reviewer verdict explicit: PASS/REWORK/REJECT.

## Reporting Constraint
Do not claim readiness/quality without command output or measurable artifacts.

---

# Project Rules (Global Workspace: C:\Users\serge)

## Scope
These rules apply to work performed under `C:\Users\serge` unless a project-level `AGENTS.md` overrides with stricter requirements.

## Execution Flow
1. Do a brief context and risk check.
2. Classify the change as `TRIVIAL`, `MINOR`, `MAJOR`, or `CRITICAL` when it is more than a micro-change.
3. For normal local work, execute without waiting for approval.
4. Keep the plan short unless the task is risky, architectural, or cross-cutting.
5. Verify with the smallest sufficient command artifacts for the change class.
6. Report `Implemented` and `Verified` separately.

## Rule Precedence
- User instructions narrow the task, but do not bypass safety, security, or verification gates.
- Project-level `AGENTS.md` files override this global profile when they are stricter or more specific.
- Skill-specific workflows apply only when the skill is triggered, and stricter skill approval gates override default autonomy.
- When rules conflict, follow the safer and more evidence-heavy rule, then call out the conflict briefly.

## Approval Boundary
Ask before proceeding only when one of these is true:
- destructive commands or destructive cleanup
- production deploys or production infrastructure changes
- database/schema migrations or irreversible data operations
- secret creation, replacement, or exposure risk
- force push, hard reset, history rewrite
- external side effects that contact or mutate third-party systems: sending email or chat messages, creating/updating/deleting issues or tickets, changing CRM/Notion/Sheets records, resolving incidents, purchasing domains, payments, or customer-visible actions
- installing or upgrading external tools/packages globally, or changing pinned tool versions, unless the user explicitly asked for that installation/update
- browser automation that reuses the user's logged-in browser/session, creates persistent profiles, solves captchas, bypasses anti-bot systems, or performs account actions
- edits outside the intended workspace or to user/system files unrelated to the task

## Safe Lane (Default)
- Map dependencies (imports in/out where applicable).
- Read related files before edits.
- List risks (API contracts, schemas, types, security).
- Keep rollback strategy explicit.
- Use available tools proactively when they reduce uncertainty or speed execution.
- If the worktree is dirty, do not revert unrelated changes. Work only in the intended files and report unexpected overlap.

## Fast Lane (Micro-change)
Allowed only when all are true:
- 1-2 files touched.
- No public API contract change.
- No DB schema/migration change.
- No auth/security policy change.

Fast Lane flow:
1. 3-line micro-plan.
2. Execute immediately.
3. Report `Implemented` and `Verified` separately.

## Fast Track Trigger
If the user writes `[FAST]`, move after a minimal context check straight to execution unless the task crosses the Approval Boundary.

## Output Contract
Always separate:
- `Implemented`: exact file/config/code changes.
- `Verified`: exact commands run and outcomes.

# Security Rules

## Secret Hygiene
- Never commit real secrets, keys, or tokens.
- Use env vars and `.env.example` placeholders.
- If secret-like strings are detected, stop and ask before proceeding.

## Sensitive Data
- Do not expose PII in logs or examples.
- Use synthetic/mock values with explicit markers.

## Forbidden Without Explicit Request
- Destructive operations (`rm -rf`, hard reset, force push).
- Permission changes outside task scope.
- Unbounded destructive cleanup.
- Sending messages, resolving issues/incidents, changing third-party records, payments, purchases, or other external side effects.
- Reusing logged-in browser sessions, solving captchas, or bypassing anti-bot controls for account actions.
- Global tool/package upgrades or unpinned installs that change the agent environment.

## Safe Defaults
- Prefer parameterized queries.
- Validate external input at boundaries.
- Keep security-sensitive behavior explicit in plan and verification.
- For suspected secrets, report only the file/path and secret type. Do not quote the secret value.

---

# Standards Rules

## Command Style
- Environment is PowerShell-first on Windows.
- Prefer `Get-ChildItem`, `Select-String`, `Get-Content`, `Set-Content`.
- Prefer `rg` for fast search when available.
- Avoid bash-only executable command examples in this workspace.

## Change Discipline
- Keep edits minimal and scoped to task intent.
- Avoid large blind replacements.
- Do not claim KPI/coverage/quality without artifacts.
- For reviews, prioritize findings (bugs, regressions, risks, missing tests).

## Verification Baseline
Before finalizing changes:
1. Run smoke check.
2. Run stack-specific checks if applicable:
   - JS/TS: lint, test, typecheck.
   - Python: lint, test, typecheck.
   - .NET: restore, build, test.
3. Confirm changed file set is expected.

## Reporting
- If checks cannot run, state what is missing and why.
- Keep production path behavior unified; call out bypasses explicitly.

---

# Verification Gate Rules

## Principle
Evidence must match the claim strength.

## Evidence Marker Mapping
Use the source markers from `Integrity Rules` in final reports. Internally, map claim levels this way:
- `FACT` -> `[VERIFIED]`, `[VERIFIED-REAL]`, `[VERIFIED-SYNTHETIC]`, `[DOCS]`, or `[CODE]`.
- `INFERENCE` -> `[INFERRED]`, with the verified facts that support it.
- `HYPOTHESIS` -> `[HYPOTHESIS]`, never as a confirmed finding.
- `SYNTHETIC RESULT` -> `[VERIFIED-SYNTHETIC]`, and downgrade real-world readiness to `[NEEDS-REAL-DATA]`.

## Claim Levels
- `FACT`: directly verified by code, docs, command output, test output, logs, or browser/UI observation.
- `INFERENCE`: reasoned from verified facts; include the chain.
- `HYPOTHESIS`: plausible but not verified; requires more checking before action or user-facing certainty.
- `SYNTHETIC RESULT`: checked only on mock/generated/demo data; useful for mechanics, not production validation.

## Required Verification
- Bug claims: reproduce with an error, failing test, log, trace, or concrete code path.
- Security claims: identify trust boundary, input source, sink, and exploitability or mitigation.
- API/CLI/config claims: verify against local code, installed help output, or official docs.
- UI claims: verify with browser/screenshot when the UI can run.
- Performance/accuracy claims: use real or pre-existing benchmark data; synthetic data must be labeled.

## Downgrade Rules
- If tests pass against the relevant existing suite, downgrade unproven bug claims to `[HYPOTHESIS]`.
- If only synthetic checks were run, downgrade production-readiness claims to `[NEEDS-REAL-DATA]`.
- If sources conflict, label `[CONFLICTING]` and state both sides.
- If verification cannot run, state why and report the safest remaining evidence.

## Output Rule
Final reports must keep `Implemented` separate from `Verified`, and must not claim readiness, correctness, coverage, or safety beyond the verification actually performed.

20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:194:    WHY: Z0 = Z* T with Z* an exact orthonormal kernel basis, T = Z*^T Z0, T^T T = I + E, ||E|| <= delta.
20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:195:    Then ||T^-1||^2 <= 1/(1 - delta), and every quantity of the bound (lambda_K, ||M Z||, kappa_K) computed
20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:196:    from Z0 is at most 1/(1 - delta) times the exact value; hence B_true <= B_code / (1 - delta).

58:def real_form_of_hermitian_part_arb(Rr):
59:    """Real 40x40 form [[A, -B], [B, A]] of H = (R + R^H)/2 with A = Re H, B = Im H, all in arb.
60:
61:    Re H_ij = (Re R_ij + Re R_ji)/2,  Im H_ij = (Im R_ij - Im R_ji)/2. Exact sums of doubles in ball arithmetic,
62:    so the enclosure contains the exact Hermitian part of the file's R (no float rounding).
63:    """
64:    n = Rr.shape[0]
65:    re = [
66:        [(arb(float(Rr[i, j].real)) + arb(float(Rr[j, i].real))) / 2 for j in range(n)]
67:        for i in range(n)
68:    ]
69:    im = [
70:        [(arb(float(Rr[i, j].imag)) - arb(float(Rr[j, i].imag))) / 2 for j in range(n)]
71:        for i in range(n)
72:    ]
73:    entries = []
74:    for i in range(2 * n):
75:        for j in range(2 * n):
76:            if i < n and j < n:
77:                entries.append(re[i][j])
78:            elif i < n:
79:                entries.append(-im[i][j - n])
80:            elif j < n:
81:                entries.append(im[i - n][j])
82:            else:
83:                entries.append(re[i - n][j - n])
84:    return arb_mat(2 * n, 2 * n, entries)

95:def np_of(B):
96:    n, m = B.nrows(), B.ncols()
97:    return np.array([[float(B[i, j].mid()) for j in range(m)] for i in range(n)])

100:def lam_max_upper(B):
101:    """Rigorous upper bound (arb) for lambda_max of a symmetric arb matrix B (Gershgorin on V^-1 B V)."""
102:    n = B.nrows()
103:    Bf = np_of(B)
104:    Bf = 0.5 * (Bf + Bf.T)
105:    _, V = np.linalg.eigh(Bf)
106:    Vb = to_arb_mat(V)
107:    C = Vb.inv() * B * Vb
108:    best = None
109:    for i in range(n):
110:        up = (C[i, i] + sum((abs(C[i, j]) for j in range(n) if j != i), arb(0))).upper()
111:        if best is None or float(up) > float(best):
112:            best = up
113:    return best

127:def contains_zero(x):

129:    return lo(x) <= 0.0 <= hi(x)

132:def build_cell(cell):
133:    """Exact (ball) centre, Phi0, G, pseudo-inverse data, projector P and kernel basis Z0 for one cell."""
134:    th_lo, th_hi, ph_lo, ph_hi = (float(v) for v in cell)
135:    a_tl, a_th, a_pl, a_ph = arb(th_lo), arb(th_hi), arb(ph_lo), arb(ph_hi)
136:    th_c, ph_c = (a_tl + a_th) / 2, (a_pl + a_ph) / 2
137:    hth, hph = (a_th - a_tl) / 2, (a_ph - a_pl) / 2
138:    du = (hth / 2 + (a_th / 2).sin() * hph + arb(1e-12)).upper()
139:    u0 = [(th_c / 2).cos(), arb(0), (th_c / 2).sin() * ph_c.cos(), (th_c / 2).sin() * ph_c.sin()]
140:    Phi0 = PHI[0] * u0[0]
141:    for j in range(1, 4):
142:        Phi0 = Phi0 + PHI[j] * u0[j]
143:    G = Phi0 * Phi0.transpose()
144:    Pinv = Phi0.transpose() * G.inv()  # 40 x 36
145:    P = I40 - Pinv * Phi0
146:    # WHY: the float SVD only seeds the starting vectors; P projects them exactly onto ker Phi0
147:    u0f = [float(t.mid()) for t in u0]
148:    Phi0f = sum(u0f[j] * PHIf[j] for j in range(4))
149:    _, _, Vt = np.linalg.svd(Phi0f)

153:    Q = []
154:    for k in range(4):
155:        v = cols[k][:]
156:        for q in Q:
157:            d = sum((q[i] * v[i] for i in range(40)), arb(0))
158:            v = [v[i] - d * q[i] for i in range(40)]
159:        nrm = sum((x * x for x in v), arb(0)).sqrt()
160:        Q.append([x / nrm for x in v])
161:    Z0 = arb_mat(40, 4, [Q[k][i] for i in range(40) for k in range(4)])
162:    return {"du": du, "Phi0": Phi0, "G": G, "Pinv": Pinv, "P": P, "Z0": Z0}

165:def kernel_certificate(Phi0, G, P, Z0):
166:    """Return the list of failed certificate items (empty list = certified). Checks (a)-(d) in the module docstring."""
167:    fails = []
168:    d = G.det()
169:    if not (lo(d) > 0.0):
170:        fails.append("a: det(G) not certified > 0 (Phi0 not certified full row rank 36)")
171:    R0 = Phi0 * Z0
172:    if not all(contains_zero(R0[i, k]) for i in range(R0.nrows()) for k in range(R0.ncols())):
173:        fails.append("b: Phi0*Z0 does not contain 0 (columns not certified in kernel)")
174:    Gm = Z0.transpose() * Z0
175:    for i in range(4):
176:        s = sum(
177:            (hi(abs(Gm[i, j] - (arb(1) if i == j else arb(0)))) for j in range(4)),
178:            0.0,
179:        )
180:        if not (s < 1.0):
181:            fails.append(
182:                "c: Gram Z0^T Z0 - I not certified < 1 (columns not certified independent)"
183:            )
184:            break
185:    PP = Phi0 * P
186:    if not all(contains_zero(PP[i, j]) for i in range(PP.nrows()) for j in range(PP.ncols())):
187:        fails.append("d: Phi0*P does not contain 0 (projector not certified)")
188:    return fails

191:def gram_defect(Z0):
192:    """Certified upper bound (arb) delta >= ||Z0^T Z0 - I||_2, via Gershgorin row sums in ball arithmetic.
193:
194:    WHY: Z0 = Z* T with Z* an exact orthonormal kernel basis, T = Z*^T Z0, T^T T = I + E, ||E|| <= delta.
195:    Then ||T^-1||^2 <= 1/(1 - delta), and every quantity of the bound (lambda_K, ||M Z||, kappa_K) computed
196:    from Z0 is at most 1/(1 - delta) times the exact value; hence B_true <= B_code / (1 - delta).
197:    """
198:    Gm = Z0.transpose() * Z0
199:    rows = [
200:        sum((abs(Gm[i, j] - (arb(1) if i == j else arb(0))) for j in range(4)), arb(0)).upper()
201:        for i in range(4)
202:    ]
203:    d = rows[0]
204:    for r in rows[1:]:
205:        if float(r) > float(d):
206:            d = r
207:    return d

210:def cell_bound(cell):
211:    """Certified bound for one cell: {'status': 'OK', 'B_up': arb, ...} or {'status': 'CERT_FAIL'|'KAPPA_GE_1', ...}."""
212:    b = build_cell(cell)
213:    fails = kernel_certificate(b["Phi0"], b["G"], b["P"], b["Z0"])
214:    if fails:
215:        return {"status": "CERT_FAIL", "reasons": fails}
216:    du, Pinv, Z0 = b["du"], b["Pinv"], b["Z0"]
217:    Z0t = Z0.transpose()
218:    A = [Pinv * PHI[j] for j in range(4)]  # 40 x 40
219:    t_full = [lam_max_upper(A[j].transpose() * A[j]) for j in range(4)]
220:    t_K = []
221:    for j in range(4):
222:        X = A[j] * Z0
223:        t_K.append(lam_max_upper(X.transpose() * X))
224:    kappa = (du * sum((t for t in t_full), arb(0)).sqrt()).upper()
225:    kappa_K = (du * sum((t for t in t_K), arb(0)).sqrt()).upper()
226:    lam_h = lam_max_upper(Z0t * Mb * Z0)
227:    # WHY: the test must use the UPPER bound of lambda_K, not its midpoint; max(lambda_K, 0) <= max(upper, 0)
228:    lam_up = lam_h.upper()
229:    lamK = lam_up if float(lam_up) > 0 else arb(0)
230:    mz = lam_max_upper(Z0t * M2 * Z0).sqrt().upper()
231:    one_minus = arb(1) - kappa
232:    if not (one_minus > 0):
233:        return {"status": "KAPPA_GE_1"}
234:    rho = (kappa_K / one_minus).upper()
235:    B = (lamK + 2 * mz * rho + mn_up * rho * rho).upper()
236:    delta = gram_defect(Z0)
237:    one_minus_delta = arb(1) - delta
238:    if not (one_minus_delta > 0):
239:        return {
240:            "status": "CERT_FAIL",
241:            "reasons": ["c: delta >= 1 (non-orthonormality not controlled)"],
242:        }
243:    B_cert = (B / one_minus_delta).upper()  # certified bound for an exact orthonormal kernel basis
244:    return {
245:        "status": "OK",
246:        "B_up": B_cert,
247:        "B_code": B,
248:        "delta": delta,
249:        "kappa": kappa,
250:        "rho": rho,
251:        "lamK": lamK,
252:        "mz": mz,
253:    }

31:
32:def _matrix(rows, cols, fn):
33:    return arb_mat(rows, cols, [fn(i, j) for i in range(rows) for j in range(cols)])
34:
35:
36:def _variant_z(Z0, fn):
37:    return _matrix(40, 4, lambda i, k: fn(i, k, Z0[i, k]))
38:

40:def main():
41:    rows_top = sorted(json.load(open(TOP, encoding="utf-8"))["rows"], key=lambda r: r["slack"])
42:    leaves = np.load(LEAVES, mmap_mode="r")
43:    rng = np.random.default_rng(7)
44:    rand_idx = sorted(rng.choice(len(leaves), size=100, replace=False).tolist())
45:
46:    test_cells = [(r["cell"], r["B_up"]) for r in rows_top[:200]]
47:    test_cells += [(leaves[i].tolist(), None) for i in rand_idx]
48:
49:    report = {
50:        "R_hermitian_exact": ivl.R_HERMITIAN_EXACT,
51:        "final_gap_positive": bool(ivl.final_gap > 0),
52:        "final_gap": float(ivl.final_gap),
53:        "s_target": float(ivl.s_target_f),
54:        "positive_control": {},
55:        "negative_tests": {},
56:    }
57:
58:    certified = 0
59:    slack_ok = 0
60:    cert_fail = []
61:    max_diff_v1 = 0.0
62:    min_slack = None
63:    for k, (cell, v1_bup) in enumerate(test_cells):
64:        r = ivl.cell_bound([float(x) for x in cell])
65:        if r["status"] != "OK":
66:            cert_fail.append(
67:                {"idx": k, "cell": cell, "status": r["status"], "reasons": r.get("reasons")}
68:            )
69:            continue
70:        certified += 1
71:        if ivl.s_target - r["B_up"] > 0:
72:            slack_ok += 1
73:        sl = float(ivl.s_target - r["B_up"])
74:        min_slack = sl if min_slack is None else min(min_slack, sl)
75:        if v1_bup is not None:
76:            max_diff_v1 = max(max_diff_v1, abs(float(r["B_up"]) - float(v1_bup)))
77:    report["positive_control"] = {
78:        "cells": len(test_cells),
79:        "certified": certified,
80:        "slack_positive": slack_ok,
81:        "cert_fail_sample": cert_fail[:20],
82:        "min_slack": min_slack,
83:        "max_abs_diff_B_up_vs_v1_top200": max_diff_v1,
84:    }
85:
86:    # Negative tests on the cell with the smallest stored slack (kernel and lambda_K must not be certified)
87:    cell0 = [float(x) for x in rows_top[0]["cell"]]
88:    b = ivl.build_cell(cell0)
89:    Phi0, G, P, Z0 = b["Phi0"], b["G"], b["P"], b["Z0"]
90:    neg = report["negative_tests"]
91:
92:    def record(name, fails, extra=None):
93:        neg[name] = {"rejected": bool(fails), "reasons": fails}
94:        if extra:
95:            neg[name].update(extra)
96:
97:    # N0: genuine data must pass (control for the negatives)
98:    record("N0_genuine_control", ivl.kernel_certificate(Phi0, G, P, Z0))
99:
100:    # N1: one kernel column replaced by a unit vector (not in ker Phi0)
101:    Z_n1 = _variant_z(
102:        Z0, lambda i, k, v: arb(1) if (i == 0 and k == 0) else (arb(0) if k == 0 else v)
103:    )
104:    record("N1_column_not_in_kernel", ivl.kernel_certificate(Phi0, G, P, Z_n1))
105:
106:    # N2: two identical columns (dependent basis)
107:    Z_n2 = _variant_z(Z0, lambda i, k, v: Z0[i, 0] if k == 1 else v)
108:    record("N2_dependent_columns", ivl.kernel_certificate(Phi0, G, P, Z_n2))
109:
110:    # N3: basis scaled by 2 (not orthonormal; lambda_K computed from it would be off by a factor 4)
111:    Z_n3 = _variant_z(Z0, lambda i, k, v: 2 * v)
112:    record("N3_not_orthonormal_scaled", ivl.kernel_certificate(Phi0, G, P, Z_n3))
113:
114:    # N4: rank-deficient Phi0 (row 35 := row 34). G singular -> certificate (a) must fail
115:    Phi_n4 = _matrix(36, 40, lambda i, j: Phi0[34, j] if i == 35 else Phi0[i, j])
116:    G_n4 = Phi_n4 * Phi_n4.transpose()
117:    det_lo = float(G_n4.det().lower())
118:    try:
119:        Pinv_n4 = Phi_n4.transpose() * G_n4.inv()
120:        P_n4 = ivl.I40 - Pinv_n4 * Phi_n4
121:        fails4 = ivl.kernel_certificate(Phi_n4, G_n4, P_n4, Z0)
122:        record("N4_rank_deficient_Phi0", fails4, {"det_lower": det_lo})
123:    except Exception as exc:  # singular inverse: the pipeline must refuse, not certify
124:        record(
125:            "N4_rank_deficient_Phi0",
126:            [f"exception while building projector: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"],
127:            {"det_lower": det_lo},
128:        )
129:
130:    # N5: zero column (rank drop of the basis)
131:    Z_n5 = _variant_z(Z0, lambda i, k, v: arb(0) if k == 2 else v)
132:    record("N5_zero_column", ivl.kernel_certificate(Phi0, G, P, Z_n5))
133:
134:    neg_ok = (
135:        all(v["rejected"] for key, v in neg.items() if key != "N0_genuine_control")
136:        and not neg["N0_genuine_control"]["rejected"]
137:    )
138:    report["negatives_all_rejected_and_control_passes"] = bool(neg_ok)
139:    # WHY: v2 uses the exact (arb) Hermitian part of R, v1 the float one: B_up may differ at ~1e-16 level only
140:    pos_ok = certified == len(test_cells) and slack_ok == len(test_cells) and max_diff_v1 <= 1e-12
141:    report["positive_control_all_pass"] = bool(pos_ok)
142:    report["verdict"] = (
143:        "PREFLIGHT_OK" if (pos_ok and neg_ok and report["final_gap_positive"]) else "PREFLIGHT_FAIL"
144:    )
145:
146:    with open(OUT, "w", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
147:        json.dump(report, fh, indent=1, default=float)
148:    print(json.dumps(report, indent=1, default=float))
149:    sys.exit(0 if report["verdict"] == "PREFLIGHT_OK" else 1)
150:
151:
152:if __name__ == "__main__":
153:    main()

1:"""Admissible-kernel span test for parameter subsets of the A0 instance (phase 2, priority P2).
2:
3:Setting (P-reg, finite POVM). For a subset S of parameter blocks and a nonzero x in C^2, put
4:    Y_S(x) = { y in C^20 : A_i^dag y = lambda_i x for all i in S, with real lambda_i }.
5:Each row of Phi_x (real-linear in y = (Re y, Im y) in R^40) encodes three real conditions per block:
6:    Re(g1 . y) = 0, Im(g1 . y) = 0, Im(g3 . y) = 0,   g1 = x0 a1 - x1 a0,  g3 = conj(x0) a0 + conj(x1) a1,
7:with a_k = conj(A[:, k]), because A^dag y = (a0 . y, a1 . y).
8:A saturating finite POVM (P-reg) has every kernel part y_w in Y_S := union over x of Y_S(x), and
9:sum_w y_w y_w^dag = I_20. Hence span_C(Y_S) must equal C^20 (necessary condition, "admissible span").
10:
11:Procedure per subset S:
12:  1. Generic sampling: random unit x in C^2, kernel of Phi_x (real basis), convert to complex vectors.
13:  2. Special points: scan (theta, phi) with x = (cos theta, sin theta e^{i phi}) on a grid, locate local minima of
14:     sigma_min(Phi_x), refine by Nelder-Mead, keep points where the smallest singular value vanishes.
15:  3. Complex rank of all kernel vectors (sampled + special). Full rank 20 means the test does not obstruct S.
16:Also records the kernel-block generator rank (C2 premise) for comparison.
17:
18:Usage (absolute path to instance file):
19:    python -I s8_admissible_span.py <instance.jsonl> <mode> [subset-size list]
20:    mode: prefix  -> prefixes s = 1..16
21:          sizes   -> all subsets with sizes given (e.g. 13 14 15)

35:def load_A(path):
36:    with open(path, encoding="utf-8") as fh:
37:        rec = json.loads(fh.readline())
38:    blocks = []
39:    for flat in rec["blocks"]:
40:        re = np.array([[flat[2 * r + a] for a in range(2)] for r in range(20)], dtype=float)
41:        im = np.array([[flat[40 + 2 * r + a] for a in range(2)] for r in range(20)], dtype=float)
42:        blocks.append(-2j * (re + 1j * im))
43:    return blocks
44:
45:
46:def phi_rows(As, S, x):
47:    """Real matrix (3|S| x 40) of the admissibility constraints for x in C^2."""
48:    rows = []
49:    for i in S:
50:        A = As[i]
51:        a0 = np.conj(A[:, 0])
52:        a1 = np.conj(A[:, 1])
53:        g1 = x[0] * a1 - x[1] * a0
54:        g3 = np.conj(x[0]) * a0 + np.conj(x[1]) * a1
55:        rows.append(np.concatenate([g1.real, -g1.imag]))  # Re(g1.y)
56:        rows.append(np.concatenate([g1.imag, g1.real]))  # Im(g1.y)
57:        rows.append(np.concatenate([g3.imag, g3.real]))  # Im(g3.y)
58:    return np.array(rows)
59:
60:
61:def x_from_angles(theta, phi):
62:    return np.array([np.cos(theta), np.sin(theta) * np.exp(1j * phi)])
63:
64:
65:def kernel_complex(Phi, tol):
66:    _, sv, Vh = np.linalg.svd(Phi)
67:    # Phi has 40 columns; singular values beyond len(sv) are zero by definition
68:    full = np.zeros(Phi.shape[1])
69:    full[: len(sv)] = sv
70:    null_idx = np.where(full < tol)[0]
71:    vecs = []
72:    for k in null_idx:
73:        r = Vh[k]
74:        vecs.append(r[:N_KER] + 1j * r[N_KER:])
75:    return vecs, full
76:
77:
78:def sigma_min(As, S, theta, phi):
79:    """m-th singular value of Phi_x with m = min(3|S|, 40): it vanishes exactly when the rank drops below m,
80:    i.e. when the admissible kernel jumps above its generic dimension (generic dimension is 40 - 3|S| when 3|S| < 40)."""

38:## 4. Полюс в лемме о ядре (P2)
50:## 5. Лемма ячейки (P3)
52:Центр u₀ = (cos θ_c/2, 0, sin θ_c/2 cos φ_c, sin θ_c/2 sin φ_c) ∈ S³, полуширины h_θ, h_φ; du := ½h_θ + sin(θ_hi/2)h_φ (+10⁻¹² в коде). Φ₀ := Φ(u₀), A_j := Φ₀⁺Φ_j, κ := du(Σ‖A_j‖²)^{1/2}, Z — ортонормированный базис K := ker Φ₀, κ_K := du(Σ‖A_jZ‖²)^{1/2}, ρ := κ_K/(1−κ), B := max(λ_K,0) + 2‖MZ‖ρ + ‖M‖ρ², λ_K := λ_max(ZᵀMZ), M — вещественная форма R.
54:- **L1 (радиус).** |∂_θu| = ½ и |∂_φu| = sin(θ/2) (прямой расчёт). Путь «сначала θ, потом φ» даёт |u − u₀| ≤ ½h_θ + sin(θ_hi/2)h_φ = du для всех точек ячейки.
55:- **L2 (шар на сфере).** Для любого единичного u с |u − u₀| ≤ du и любого единичного y ∈ ker Φ(u): Φ₀y = −Φ(Δ)y, Δ := u − u₀. Лемма верна для всех таких u, не только для u из ячейки.
56:- **L3 (разложение).** y = z + w, z ∈ K, w ∈ row(Φ₀). Φ₀w = −Φ(Δ)y, поэтому w = −Φ₀⁺Φ(Δ)y. По Коши–Шварцу ‖Φ₀⁺Φ(Δ)v‖ ≤ du(Σ‖A_j‖²)^{1/2}‖v‖ = κ‖v‖ и для v = Zc: ≤ κ_K|c|. Отсюда |w| ≤ κ_K|z| + κ|w|, т.е. |w| ≤ ρ|z| при κ < 1. Ранг Φ₀ полный (σ₃₆(Φ₀) > 0) нужен для Φ₀⁺, а не в самой точке u. Вырожденные точки u (ядро прыгает) не требуют отдельного рассмотрения.
57:- **L4 (форма).** |z|²+|w|² = 1, |c| = |z| ≤ 1. yᵀMy = zᵀMz + 2zᵀMw + wᵀMw ≤ max(λ_K,0) + 2‖MZ‖ρ + ‖M‖ρ² = B.

129:| tr R − 20s > 0 | §7 | arb | 0.0304052453 | VERIFIED-interval |
130:| Все листы B_up < s | §8, §10 | журналы `o3_v2` + arb | 3 955 841, 0 отказов | VERIFIED-interval (o3_v2) |
132:| Полнота покрытия | §4 P4 | `verify_tiling_exact.py` (+ 2 контрольных случая) | точное совпадение с деревом бисекции, 5 274 284 узла | VERIFIED-exact |
1:# O3 v2 — закрытие: округления, оценки, покрытие, происхождение журналов (2026-10-31)
2:
3:Объект: верхняя граница B_up(cell) < s для всех ячеек направлений x ∈ S³ (Bloch-параметризация) при S− = {4..15}, и разделитель tr R − 20 s > 0.
4:
5:## 1. Что именно доказывается
6:
7:Для каждой ячейки Λ (направления x с |u − u₀| ≤ du) и каждого y ∈ ker Φ(u), |y| = 1: yᴴRy ≤ B_up(Λ) ≤ s. Из этого по разделению (`STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md` §6) tr R ≤ 20 sup ≤ 20 s, что противоречит tr R − 20 s > 0. Итоговая величина: tr R − 20 s = 0.030405245308261095 (arb, 192 бита).
8:
9:## 2. Аналитические оценки (cell lemma L1–L4)
10:
11:- L1: |u − u₀| ≤ du для всех u в ячейке (путь «θ, потом φ»; формула du = ½h_θ + sin(θ_hi/2)·h_φ + 10⁻¹² — верхняя граница в arb).
12:- L2: для u с |u − u₀| ≤ du и единичного y ∈ ker Φ(u): ‖Φ₀ y‖ = ‖Φ(Δ)y‖ ≤ du·(Σ‖A_j‖²)^{1/2}.
13:- L3: разложение y = z + w, z ∈ K = ker Φ₀, w ∈ row Φ₀; |w| ≤ ρ|z| при κ < 1 (ρ = κ_K/(1−κ)).
14:- L4: yᵀMy ≤ max(λ_K, 0) + 2‖MZ‖ρ + ‖M‖ρ² = B.
15:Статус: **PROVED-hand** (перевыведено в `STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md` §5; агент-аудит O1 — частично, слабая независимость). Математика проста; коэффициенты — явные.
16:
17:## 3. Округления
18:
19:- Вычисления в arb (ball arithmetic), prec 192 бита. Все операции — с сохранением включения (inclusion isotonicity): exact значение лежит внутри шара.
20:- Входы: R (float64, точные двоичные рациональные числа), границы ячеек (float), константа 10⁻¹² (верхняя граница). Преобразование R: симметризация в arb, (R + Rᴴ)/2, — ошибка 5·10⁻¹⁷ в исходной матрице переносится на tr R на 10⁻¹⁶, что много меньше зазора 0.0304.
21:- Границы: λ_max верхняя (Gershgorin на V⁻¹BV, V — любой преобразователь из float eigh; корректность не зависит от точности V, так как Gershgorin — для спектра C = V⁻¹BV, а C вычисляется в arb).
22:- s_target = 0.9·tr R/20: вычислена из float-значений ((arb(float)) — точное значение в binary, без округления при переходе.
23:- **Минимальный запас** в прогоне 8.4·10⁻⁹ — это ширина шара, в котором arb гарантирует включение; порядок ошибки округления при 192 битах около 10⁻⁵⁸. То есть запас 8.4·10⁻⁹ — не эвристика, а строго положительный интервальный результат.
24:

Контроли: удаление листа даёт непокрытые клетки; дубликат — ошибку. Покрывается прямоугольник [0,π_f]×[0,2π_f]; остальное — лемма L2 (полюс).

1:"""O3 v2: full certified pass over all accepted leaves of S- with interval_cells_v2 (kernel certificate).
2:
3:Input : inputs/leaves.npy (SHA-256 checked against the stage-C manifest before the run)
4:Output: metrics/o3_progress.jsonl  (one line per chunk: chunk, n, ok, fails, elapsed)
5:        metrics/o3_failures.jsonl  (one line per failed leaf: leaf index, cell, status, reasons)
6:        metrics/o3_summary.json    (only after all chunks are in the progress file)
7:Resume: chunks already present in o3_progress.jsonl are skipped; a chunk is written only after all its leaves
8:        were processed, so an interrupted chunk is redone and never double counted.
9:Usage : python -I code/o3_run_v2.py [n_workers]     (default 8; BELOW_NORMAL priority; BLAS = 1 per worker)

38:def _init_worker():
39:    """Windows BELOW_NORMAL priority so the desktop stays responsive."""
40:    try:
41:        import psutil
42:
43:        psutil.Process().nice(psutil.BELOW_NORMAL_PRIORITY_CLASS)
44:    except Exception:
45:        pass

48:def _load():
49:    global _ivl
50:    if _ivl is None:
51:        sys.path.insert(0, HERE)
52:        import interval_cells_v2 as ivl  # noqa: E402
53:
54:        _ivl = ivl
55:    return _ivl
56:
57:
58:def _sha256(path):
59:    h = hashlib.sha256()
60:    with open(path, "rb") as fh:
61:        for block in iter(lambda: fh.read(1 << 20), b""):
62:            h.update(block)
63:    return h.hexdigest()
64:
65:
66:def _done_chunks(path):
67:    done = set()
68:    if os.path.exists(path):
69:        with open(path, encoding="utf-8") as fh:
70:            for line in fh:
71:                if line.strip():
72:                    done.add(json.loads(line)["chunk"])
73:    return done
74:
75:
76:def work(args):
77:    cid, base, cells = args
78:    ivl = _load()
79:    s = ivl.s_target
80:    n_ok = 0
81:    fails = []
82:    min_slack = None
83:    max_bup = None
84:    for k, c in enumerate(cells):
85:        leaf_index = base + k
86:        cell = [float(v) for v in c]
87:        try:
88:            r = ivl.cell_bound(cell)
89:        except Exception as exc:  # per leaf: record, never let one leaf kill the chunk or the pool
90:            fails.append(
91:                {
92:                    "leaf": leaf_index,
93:                    "cell": cell,
94:                    "status": "EXCEPTION",
95:                    "reasons": [f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"],
96:                }
97:            )
98:            continue
99:        if r["status"] != "OK":
100:            fails.append(
101:                {
102:                    "leaf": leaf_index,
103:                    "cell": cell,
104:                    "status": r["status"],
105:                    "reasons": r.get("reasons", []),
106:                }
107:            )
108:            continue
109:        b = r["B_up"]
110:        sl = float(s - b)
111:        bf = float(b)
112:        min_slack = sl if min_slack is None else min(min_slack, sl)
113:        max_bup = bf if max_bup is None else max(max_bup, bf)
114:        if s - b > 0:  # arb comparison: true only if certainly positive
115:            n_ok += 1
116:        else:
117:            fails.append(
118:                {
119:                    "leaf": leaf_index,
120:                    "cell": cell,
121:                    "status": "SLACK_NOT_POSITIVE",
122:                    "reasons": [f"B_up={bf!r} >= s={float(s)!r}"],
123:                }
124:            )
125:    return cid, len(cells), n_ok, fails, min_slack, max_bup

128:def main():
129:    n_workers = int(sys.argv[1]) if len(sys.argv) > 1 else DEFAULT_WORKERS
130:    t_start = time.time()
131:    leaves_sha = _sha256(LEAVES)
132:    leaves = np.load(LEAVES, mmap_mode="r")
133:    N = len(leaves)

135:    done = _done_chunks(PROGRESS)
136:    todo = [c for c in range(total_chunks) if c not in done]
137:    chunks = [(c, c * CHUNK, np.asarray(leaves[c * CHUNK : (c + 1) * CHUNK])) for c in todo]
138:    ivl = _load()
139:    print(
140:        f"leaves {N} sha256 {leaves_sha}; chunks total {total_chunks}, done {len(done)}, "
141:        f"to do {len(chunks)}, workers {n_workers}; final gap trR-20s > 0: {bool(ivl.final_gap > 0)}",
142:        flush=True,
143:    )
144:    t0 = time.time()
145:    tot_ok = 0
146:    tot_n = 0
147:    n_fail = 0
148:    min_slack = None
149:    max_bup = None
150:    with (
151:        Pool(n_workers, initializer=_init_worker) as pool,
152:        open(PROGRESS, "a", encoding="utf-8") as pf,
153:        open(FAILURES, "a", encoding="utf-8") as ff,
154:    ):
155:        for cid, n, n_ok, fails, mins, maxb in pool.imap_unordered(work, chunks, chunksize=1):
156:            tot_n += n
157:            tot_ok += n_ok
158:            n_fail += len(fails)
159:            for f in fails:
160:                ff.write(json.dumps({"chunk": cid, **f}) + "\n")
161:            ff.flush()
162:            if mins is not None:
163:                min_slack = mins if min_slack is None else min(min_slack, mins)
164:            if maxb is not None:
165:                max_bup = maxb if max_bup is None else max(max_bup, maxb)
166:            pf.write(
167:                json.dumps(
168:                    {
169:                        "chunk": cid,
170:                        "n": n,
171:                        "ok": n_ok,
172:                        "fails": len(fails),
173:                        "elapsed_s": round(time.time() - t0, 1),
174:                    }
175:                )
176:                + "\n"
177:            )
178:            pf.flush()
179:            if len(fails):
180:                print(
181:                    f"chunk {cid}: {len(fails)} failed leaves, first: {fails[0]['status']}",
182:                    flush=True,
183:                )
184:            if tot_n % (CHUNK * 200) < CHUNK:
185:                print(
186:                    f"processed {tot_n} leaves this run, ok {tot_ok}, fails {n_fail}, {time.time() - t0:.0f}s",
187:                    flush=True,
188:                )
189:
190:    # coverage check against the progress file (each chunk exactly once)
191:    seen = []
192:    with open(PROGRESS, encoding="utf-8") as fh:
193:        for line in fh:
194:            if line.strip():
195:                seen.append(json.loads(line)["chunk"])
196:    covered = set(seen)
197:    leaves_in_progress = 0
198:    with open(PROGRESS, encoding="utf-8") as fh:
199:        for line in fh:
200:            if line.strip():
201:                leaves_in_progress += json.loads(line)["n"]
202:    summary = {
203:        "leaves_total": N,
204:        "chunks_total": total_chunks,
205:        "chunk_lines_in_progress": len(seen),
206:        "chunks_unique_in_progress": len(covered),
207:        "duplicate_chunk_lines": len(seen) - len(covered),
208:        "all_chunks_covered_once": bool(len(covered) == total_chunks and len(seen) == total_chunks),
209:        "leaves_counted_in_progress": leaves_in_progress,
210:        "leaves_match_total": bool(leaves_in_progress == N),
211:        "ok_this_run": tot_ok,
212:        "failed_this_run": n_fail,
213:        "final_gap_trR_minus_20s": float(ivl.final_gap),
214:        "final_gap_positive": bool(ivl.final_gap > 0),
215:        "s_target": float(ivl.s_target_f),
216:        "min_slack_this_run": min_slack,
217:        "max_B_up_this_run": max_bup,
218:        "leaves_sha256": leaves_sha,
219:        "prec_bits": 192,
220:        "seconds_this_run": round(time.time() - t_start, 1),
221:        "note": "failures listed in o3_failures.jsonl; a failure is never a reason to widen a tolerance",
222:    }

20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/code/verify_tiling_exact.py:1:"""Exact tiling check (stage C, item O2) for the accepted leaves of S-.
20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/code/verify_tiling_exact.py:2:
20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/code/verify_tiling_exact.py:3:Method. The leaf dump refines the root grid (16 x 32 cells from np.linspace) by splitting each
20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/code/verify_tiling_exact.py:4:failing cell at float midpoints tm = 0.5*(a+b), pm = 0.5*(c+d). Children share the same float
20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/code/verify_tiling_exact.py:5:midpoint, so each split partitions its parent exactly in real arithmetic. The accepted leaves are
20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/code/verify_tiling_exact.py:6:exactly the cells that were accepted during this refinement, so the leaf set must equal the leaves
20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/code/verify_tiling_exact.py:7:of the reconstructed partition tree. This script rebuilds that tree level by level with the same
20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/code/verify_tiling_exact.py:8:float operations and checks, with exact float-bit equality (no tolerance):
20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/code/verify_tiling_exact.py:9:
20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/code/verify_tiling_exact.py:10:  1. every accepted leaf occurs in the tree exactly once (no duplicates, nothing outside the tree);
20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/code/verify_tiling_exact.py:11:  2. every node of the tree is either an accepted leaf or is split; nothing is left uncovered
20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/code/verify_tiling_exact.py:12:     at the stopping depth.
20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/code/verify_tiling_exact.py:13:
20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/code/verify_tiling_exact.py:14:Hence the accepted leaves form an exact partition of the root rectangle [0, pi_f] x [0, 2 pi_f].
20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/code/verify_tiling_exact.py:15:Matching is on the IEEE-754 bit patterns, so this is an exact equality test.
20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/code/verify_tiling_exact.py:16:
20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/code/verify_tiling_exact.py:17:Writes metrics/tiling_exact_check.json.

## Verdict

**PASS WITH CONDITIONS — confidence 8/10.**

[CODE] The analytic kernel/cell argument is sound, the current code uses the corrected upper endpoint for \(\lambda_K\), and the Arb gap to \(\operatorname{tr}R/20\) is decisively positive.

However, the literal claim with
\[
s^*=\frac{0.9\,\operatorname{tr}R}{20}
\]
interpreted as exact real arithmetic is **not completely certified**:

- `s_target` is first rounded in binary64 and is \(1.8041124150\times10^{-18}\) **above** exact \(9\,\operatorname{tr}R/200\).
- The early 1,710,500 leaves have no stored slack values, only chunk-level `ok` counts.
- Several maximum/row-sum decisions reduce 192-bit Arb endpoints to binary64 before selecting/comparing them. This is a formal directed-rounding gap, although it is numerically tiny compared with the recorded \(8.4\times10^{-9}\) slack.

No files were changed and no full O3 pass was run.

### 1. `phi_rows` constraints — CONFIRMED

Let
\[
z=A_i^H y=(a_0\!\cdot y,a_1\!\cdot y),\qquad a_c=\overline{A_i[:,c]}.
\]
Then \(z=\lambda x\), \(\lambda\in\mathbb R\), is equivalent for \(x\ne0\) to

\[
x_0z_1-x_1z_0=0,\qquad
\operatorname{Im}(x^Hz)=0.
\]

The code uses exactly

\[
g_1=x_0a_1-x_1a_0,\qquad
g_3=\overline{x_0}a_0+\overline{x_1}a_1,
\]

and imposes \(\Re(g_1y)=0\), \(\Im(g_1y)=0\), and \(\Im(g_3y)=0\): [s8_admissible_span.py:46](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261027-h-cat56-2-discovery/code/s8_admissible_span.py:46>), [s8_admissible_span.py:51](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261027-h-cat56-2-discovery/code/s8_admissible_span.py:51>).

The real split is correct. For \(g=p+iq\), \(y=u+iv\),

\[
\Re(g\cdot y)=p\cdot u-q\cdot v,\qquad
\Im(g\cdot y)=q\cdot u+p\cdot v,
\]

matching rows `[g.real, -g.imag]` and `[g.imag, g.real]` at [s8_admissible_span.py:55](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261027-h-cat56-2-discovery/code/s8_admissible_span.py:55>).

`load_A` implements \(A_i=-2iB_i\) at [s8_admissible_span.py:35](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261027-h-cat56-2-discovery/code/s8_admissible_span.py:35>) and [s8_admissible_span.py:42](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261027-h-cat56-2-discovery/code/s8_admissible_span.py:42>).

[VERIFIED] On the supplied instance, `PHIf` has shape \(4\times36\times40\), and all its entries are exactly integral binary64 values. Direct numerical comparison of `phi_rows @ [Re y, Im y]` against the three complex conditions agreed to ordinary binary64 rounding.

The pole \(x_0=0\) and global-phase reduction are correctly handled in the proof at [STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:40](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:40>) and [STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:46](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:46>).

### 2. Cell lemmas L1–L4 — CONFIRMED, subject to item 3

The mathematical lemmas and their implementation agree.

- **L1:** With \(h_\theta=(\theta_{\rm hi}-\theta_{\rm lo})/2\) and \(h_\phi=(\phi_{\rm hi}-\phi_{\rm lo})/2\), the code computes
  \[
  du=\frac{h_\theta}{2}+\sin(\theta_{\rm hi}/2)h_\phi+\varepsilon.
  \]
  This follows from \(\|\partial_\theta u\|=1/2\) and \(\|\partial_\phi u\|=\sin(\theta/2)\). See [interval_cells_v2.py:134](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:134>) and the proof at [STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:52](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:52>).

- **L2–L3:** From \(y\in\ker\Phi(u)\),
  \[
  \Phi_0y=-\Phi(\Delta)y.
  \]
  With \(y=z+w\), \(z\in\ker\Phi_0\), \(w\in\operatorname{row}\Phi_0\),
  \[
  w=-\Phi_0^+\Phi(\Delta)y.
  \]
  The code constructs
  \[
  \Phi_0^+=\Phi_0^T(\Phi_0\Phi_0^T)^{-1},\quad
  P=I-\Phi_0^+\Phi_0
  \]
  at [interval_cells_v2.py:143](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:143>). This is the correct orthogonal row-space/kernel decomposition when \(\Phi_0\) has full row rank.

- **L4:** The implemented bound is
  \[
  B=\max(\lambda_K,0)+2\|MZ\|\rho+\|M\|\rho^2,
  \quad
  \rho=\frac{\kappa_K}{1-\kappa},
  \]
  exactly as in the proof: [interval_cells_v2.py:218](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:218>), [interval_cells_v2.py:234](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:234>), [STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:54](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:54>).

The non-orthonormality correction is conservative. If \(Z_0=ZT\) and
\(\|T^TT-I\|_2\le\delta<1\), then
\(\|T^{-1}\|^2\le(1-\delta)^{-1}\). The \(\lambda_K\), cross, and \(\rho^2\) terms therefore all acquire at most the common factor \((1-\delta)^{-1}\), which is applied at [interval_cells_v2.py:236](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:236>).

One documentation defect: an interval product “contains zero” does not, by itself, prove the exact product equals zero. Thus checks (b) and (d) at [interval_cells_v2.py:171](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:171>) and [interval_cells_v2.py:185](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:185>) are only sanity checks. Exact kernel membership follows instead from the algebraic construction \(Z_1=PZ_f\) and subsequent exact-target Gram–Schmidt at [interval_cells_v2.py:146](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:146>). With that algebraic argument, the cell lemma is valid.

### 3. Directed rounding — REFUTED as a completely rigorous implementation

Most individual enclosures are correctly directed:

- `du.upper()`: [interval_cells_v2.py:138](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:138>)
- Gershgorin row upper: [interval_cells_v2.py:110](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:110>)
- \(\kappa,\kappa_K\): [interval_cells_v2.py:224](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:224>)
- corrected \(\lambda_K\): [interval_cells_v2.py:226](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:226>)
- \(\|MZ\|\), \(\rho\), \(B\), and \(B/(1-\delta)\): [interval_cells_v2.py:230](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:230>).

But not every reduction remains in Arb:

- Gershgorin candidates are selected using `float(up) > float(best)` at [interval_cells_v2.py:111](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:111>). If two distinct 192-bit uppers round to the same binary64 value, the smaller one can be retained.
- The two candidates for \(\|M\|\) are selected by `key=float` at [interval_cells_v2.py:116](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:116>).
- The maximum Gram-defect row is likewise selected after binary64 conversion at [interval_cells_v2.py:203](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:203>).
- The kernel-certificate Gram row sums use `float(x.upper())` and binary64 summation at [interval_cells_v2.py:175](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:175>).
- `float(lam_up) > 0` at [interval_cells_v2.py:229](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:229>) can theoretically lose an extremely small positive value through underflow.

These are formal soundness gaps, even though any resulting error is at binary64-ulp scale and is numerically far below the recorded slack.

Float-to-Arb conversions:

| Conversion | Exactness |
|---|---|
| `arb(float(v))` for `PHIf`, eigenvector `V`, and SVD seed `Vt` | Exact conversion of the resulting binary64 number; [interval_cells_v2.py:50](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:50>) |
| Stored `R_raw.real/imag` to Arb | Exact binary64 injection; [interval_cells_v2.py:65](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:65>) |
| Cell edges `float64 → Python float → arb` | Exact preservation of each binary64 edge; [interval_cells_v2.py:134](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:134>) |
| `arb(1e-12)` | Exact injection of the binary64 approximation, which is about \(2.01\times10^{-29}\) below decimal \(10^{-12}\); still positive and added in the correct direction |
| `arb(s_target_f)` | Exact injection of the already-rounded binary64 threshold, not exact evaluation of \(9\,\operatorname{tr}R/200\); [interval_cells_v2.py:90](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:90>) |

The NumPy eigendecomposition and SVD do not themselves need to be accurate: the eigenvectors are only an invertible similarity-transform seed, and the SVD vectors are projected by the Arb projector. See [interval_cells_v2.py:95](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:95>) and [interval_cells_v2.py:146](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:146>).

The driver’s `float(s-b)` and `float(b)` are reporting-only; acceptance uses the Arb comparison `s-b > 0` at [o3_run_v2.py:109](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/o3_run_v2.py:109>).

### 4. Strict gap and symmetrisation — REFUTED as worded; binary-target version CONFIRMED

[CONFIRMED] `final_gap > 0` is certified for the code’s binary64 `s_target_f`. `tr_exact` and `s_target` are Arb values and the comparison is performed in Arb: [interval_cells_v2.py:90](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:90>). The recorded result is \(0.030405245308261095>0\): [o3_summary.json:12](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/metrics/o3_summary.json:12>).

[REFUTED] `s_target` is not exact \(9\,\operatorname{tr}R/200\). Exact rational reconstruction from the stored diagonal gives

\[
s_{\rm float}-\frac{9\,\operatorname{tr}R}{200}
=\frac{13}{7205759403792793600}
\approx1.8041124150158794\times10^{-18}>0.
\]

Thus the run proves \(B<s_{\rm float}\), where \(s_{\rm float}\) is slightly *larger* than the exact \(s^*\). Because early leaf slacks were not stored, the primary artifacts do not prove that every early leaf also clears the exact target.

This does **not** threaten the separator conclusion itself: the certified `final_gap` proves the binary target is still strictly below \(\operatorname{tr}R/20\). It only leaves the stronger literal \(B\le s^*=0.9\,\operatorname{tr}R/20\) claim unclosed.

[CONFIRMED] The Hermitian part is formed correctly in Arb from exact binary64 components:

\[
\Re H_{ij}=\frac{\Re R_{ij}+\Re R_{ji}}2,\qquad
\Im H_{ij}=\frac{\Im R_{ij}-\Im R_{ji}}2.
\]

See [interval_cells_v2.py:58](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:58>). The raw file is not exactly Hermitian: the code records that fact at [interval_cells_v2.py:38](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:38>).

There is a stale documentation conflict: [STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:73](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:73>) says the float symmetrisation exactly equals the stored matrix, while the later correction at [STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:103](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:103>) and the code say otherwise. The actual certificate is for \(H=(R_{\rm raw}+R_{\rm raw}^H)/2\).

### 5. Covering — UNCHECKED from the listed primary artifacts alone

The mathematical phase reduction is correct: every unit \(x\) can be globally phased so \(x_0\ge0\) is real, and \(y^HRy\) is invariant under the matching phase of \(y\). The pole is covered as discussed in item 1.

The primary coverage JSON reports:

- 3,955,841 leaves;
- extrema reaching binary64 `π` and `2π`;
- total rectangle area equal to the target area.

See [leaves_coverage_2026-10-31.json:2](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/metrics/leaves_coverage_2026-10-31.json:2>) and [leaves_coverage_2026-10-31.json:19](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/metrics/leaves_coverage_2026-10-31.json:19>).

But extrema plus summed area cannot rule out a hole cancelled by an overlap. Therefore this JSON does not independently prove tiling.

The proof document reports a bit-exact reconstruction with 3,955,841 distinct matched leaves and zero uncovered cells at [STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:102](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:102>). The split argument—children sharing the same binary64 midpoint partition their parent—is sound. But the referenced verifier/output and leaf array were not among the enumerated primary artifacts, so the actual bitwise match remains `UNCHECKED` under the requested primary-only rule.

The tiny difference between real \(2\pi\) and binary64 `2*pi` is harmless if L2 is admitted: it is about \(2.45\times10^{-16}\), far below the positive \(10^{-12}\) radius enlargement.

### 6. Journaling — CONFIRMED as aggregate evidence, not per-leaf evidence

The arithmetic is exact:

- Total chunks: \(\lceil3{,}955{,}841/500\rceil=7{,}912\).
- Earlier completed chunks `0..3420`: \(3421\times500=1{,}710{,}500\) leaves.
- Resumed chunks `3421..7911`: \(2{,}245{,}341\) leaves.
- Total: \(1{,}710{,}500+2{,}245{,}341=3{,}955{,}841\).

The driver reads completed chunk IDs and skips them on resume at [o3_run_v2.py:66](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/o3_run_v2.py:66>) and [o3_run_v2.py:131](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/o3_run_v2.py:131>). Therefore `ok_this_run = 2245341` is intentionally resumed-invocation-only, not the total: [o3_summary.json:10](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/metrics/o3_summary.json:10>).

The journal audit reports exact chunk IDs, total \(n\), total `ok`, and total failures:

- 7,912 unique chunks;
- summed `n = ok = 3,955,841`;
- zero failures;
- exact-range coverage true.

See [o3_journal_audit_2026-10-31.json:2](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/metrics/o3_journal_audit_2026-10-31.json:2>) and [o3_journal_audit_2026-10-31.json:7](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/metrics/o3_journal_audit_2026-10-31.json:7>).

Exactly what is recorded:

- For every chunk: `chunk`, `n`, `ok`, `fails`, and elapsed time; [o3_run_v2.py:166](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/o3_run_v2.py:166>).
- For a failed leaf only: leaf index, cell, status, and reasons; [o3_run_v2.py:89](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/o3_run_v2.py:89>). The failure file is zero bytes.
- For the resumed invocation only: one global minimum float slack and one global maximum float `B_up`; [o3_summary.json:15](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/metrics/o3_summary.json:15>).

What is not recorded:

- No successful leaf has a per-leaf record.
- No per-leaf `B_up`, \(\kappa\), \(\rho\), \(\delta\), or Arb interval is stored.
- Early chunks `0..3420` have no stored minimum slack or maximum `B_up`.
- Later chunks also have no per-leaf bounds; only the resumed run’s two global extrema.

This limitation is explicitly acknowledged at [o3_journal_audit_2026-10-31.json:19](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/metrics/o3_journal_audit_2026-10-31.json:19>) and [O3_CLOSURE_2026-10-31.md:42](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/O3_CLOSURE_2026-10-31.md:42>).

One additional code point: the driver’s own coverage condition checks only the number of unique IDs, not equality to `range(total_chunks)`: [o3_run_v2.py:190](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/o3_run_v2.py:190>). The separate journal audit’s `cover_complete` result is therefore essential.

### 7. Historical caveats — CONFIRMED not to invalidate v2, with a provenance condition

The earlier memory exception after 3,595,000 leaves does not invalidate O3 v2. That earlier run was not used as the missing tail of the corrected v2 certificate; the proof document says a complete v2 pass was subsequently performed: [STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:81](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:81>) and [STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:103](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:103>).

The old midpoint rule for \(\lambda_K\) was not rigorous. The current code correctly uses `lam_h.upper()` before clamping against zero at [interval_cells_v2.py:226](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:226>). The current code hashes match the recorded manifest.

Condition: chunk journal records do not contain a code hash per invocation or per chunk. Therefore the assertion that early journal lines were produced by exactly the current corrected code is provenance evidence from the closure document, not something derivable from each journal record.

### 8. Dependencies outside phase4 — CONFIRMED

The running certificate is not phase4-self-contained.

- `s8_admissible_span.py` is imported from the discovery directory at [interval_cells_v2.py:32](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:32>). It supplies both `load_A` and `phi_rows`.
- The instance file is read directly from the submission-package directory at [interval_cells_v2.py:26](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:26>).
- The actual R file used by the certificate is read directly from phase3 at [interval_cells_v2.py:28](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:28>).
- Runtime correctness also depends on `python-flint`/Arb and NumPy. Importing the external `s8_admissible_span.py` additionally requires SciPy, although its optimizer is not used by O3.

[VERIFIED] The current phase3 R and external instance are byte-identical to the phase4 `o3_v2/inputs` copies whose hashes appear in the journal audit. But the driver hashes only the leaf file during the run; it does not enforce expected hashes for the actual external R, instance, `s8_admissible_span.py`, or code. The external `s8_admissible_span.py` is also absent from the stated O3 code manifest.

## Most likely failure mode and slack

The single strongest concrete failure mode is the **threshold mismatch**: the code checks against a binary64 `s_target` that is \(1.804\times10^{-18}\) above exact \(9\,\operatorname{tr}R/200\), while early per-leaf slacks were discarded. Consequently, the exact-\(s^*\) statement could in principle fail on an early cell whose slack lies between \(0\) and \(1.804\times10^{-18}\), without contradicting any stored journal record.

[INFERRED] The reported \(8.4\times10^{-9}\) minimum is enormous compared with normal 192-bit Arb roundoff: \(2^{-192}\approx1.6\times10^{-58}\) relative, so the scale separation is roughly 49–51 decimal orders. It is also about nine orders larger than binary64-ulp-scale selection errors and the exact-target mismatch. Thus Arb enclosure width is not a plausible numerical threat for the recorded closest cell. Nevertheless, precision alone does not bound width after ill-conditioned inversions, and the primary artifacts do not store the actual internal widths or early minimum slack.

The conditions for a fully clean `PASS` are:

1. Construct the threshold directly in Arb as `tr_exact * 9 / 200`.
2. Keep all maximum, row-sum, and sign decisions in Arb—no `float(...)` selection of Arb endpoints.
3. Re-certify at least chunks `0..3420`, or store chunk-level certified lower slack so the exact-target comparison can be audited.
4. Bind each journal segment to hashes of the executed certificate code, `s8_admissible_span.py`, actual external R, instance, and leaves.
5. Include the bit-exact tiling verifier/output and its leaf input in the primary certificate set.
tokens used
120 932
## Verdict

**PASS WITH CONDITIONS — confidence 8/10.**

[CODE] The analytic kernel/cell argument is sound, the current code uses the corrected upper endpoint for \(\lambda_K\), and the Arb gap to \(\operatorname{tr}R/20\) is decisively positive.

However, the literal claim with
\[
s^*=\frac{0.9\,\operatorname{tr}R}{20}
\]
interpreted as exact real arithmetic is **not completely certified**:

- `s_target` is first rounded in binary64 and is \(1.8041124150\times10^{-18}\) **above** exact \(9\,\operatorname{tr}R/200\).
- The early 1,710,500 leaves have no stored slack values, only chunk-level `ok` counts.
- Several maximum/row-sum decisions reduce 192-bit Arb endpoints to binary64 before selecting/comparing them. This is a formal directed-rounding gap, although it is numerically tiny compared with the recorded \(8.4\times10^{-9}\) slack.

No files were changed and no full O3 pass was run.

### 1. `phi_rows` constraints — CONFIRMED

Let
\[
z=A_i^H y=(a_0\!\cdot y,a_1\!\cdot y),\qquad a_c=\overline{A_i[:,c]}.
\]
Then \(z=\lambda x\), \(\lambda\in\mathbb R\), is equivalent for \(x\ne0\) to

\[
x_0z_1-x_1z_0=0,\qquad
\operatorname{Im}(x^Hz)=0.
\]

The code uses exactly

\[
g_1=x_0a_1-x_1a_0,\qquad
g_3=\overline{x_0}a_0+\overline{x_1}a_1,
\]

and imposes \(\Re(g_1y)=0\), \(\Im(g_1y)=0\), and \(\Im(g_3y)=0\): [s8_admissible_span.py:46](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261027-h-cat56-2-discovery/code/s8_admissible_span.py:46>), [s8_admissible_span.py:51](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261027-h-cat56-2-discovery/code/s8_admissible_span.py:51>).

The real split is correct. For \(g=p+iq\), \(y=u+iv\),

\[
\Re(g\cdot y)=p\cdot u-q\cdot v,\qquad
\Im(g\cdot y)=q\cdot u+p\cdot v,
\]

matching rows `[g.real, -g.imag]` and `[g.imag, g.real]` at [s8_admissible_span.py:55](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261027-h-cat56-2-discovery/code/s8_admissible_span.py:55>).

`load_A` implements \(A_i=-2iB_i\) at [s8_admissible_span.py:35](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261027-h-cat56-2-discovery/code/s8_admissible_span.py:35>) and [s8_admissible_span.py:42](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261027-h-cat56-2-discovery/code/s8_admissible_span.py:42>).

[VERIFIED] On the supplied instance, `PHIf` has shape \(4\times36\times40\), and all its entries are exactly integral binary64 values. Direct numerical comparison of `phi_rows @ [Re y, Im y]` against the three complex conditions agreed to ordinary binary64 rounding.

The pole \(x_0=0\) and global-phase reduction are correctly handled in the proof at [STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:40](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:40>) and [STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:46](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:46>).

### 2. Cell lemmas L1–L4 — CONFIRMED, subject to item 3

The mathematical lemmas and their implementation agree.

- **L1:** With \(h_\theta=(\theta_{\rm hi}-\theta_{\rm lo})/2\) and \(h_\phi=(\phi_{\rm hi}-\phi_{\rm lo})/2\), the code computes
  \[
  du=\frac{h_\theta}{2}+\sin(\theta_{\rm hi}/2)h_\phi+\varepsilon.
  \]
  This follows from \(\|\partial_\theta u\|=1/2\) and \(\|\partial_\phi u\|=\sin(\theta/2)\). See [interval_cells_v2.py:134](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:134>) and the proof at [STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:52](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:52>).

- **L2–L3:** From \(y\in\ker\Phi(u)\),
  \[
  \Phi_0y=-\Phi(\Delta)y.
  \]
  With \(y=z+w\), \(z\in\ker\Phi_0\), \(w\in\operatorname{row}\Phi_0\),
  \[
  w=-\Phi_0^+\Phi(\Delta)y.
  \]
  The code constructs
  \[
  \Phi_0^+=\Phi_0^T(\Phi_0\Phi_0^T)^{-1},\quad
  P=I-\Phi_0^+\Phi_0
  \]
  at [interval_cells_v2.py:143](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:143>). This is the correct orthogonal row-space/kernel decomposition when \(\Phi_0\) has full row rank.

- **L4:** The implemented bound is
  \[
  B=\max(\lambda_K,0)+2\|MZ\|\rho+\|M\|\rho^2,
  \quad
  \rho=\frac{\kappa_K}{1-\kappa},
  \]
  exactly as in the proof: [interval_cells_v2.py:218](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:218>), [interval_cells_v2.py:234](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:234>), [STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:54](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:54>).

The non-orthonormality correction is conservative. If \(Z_0=ZT\) and
\(\|T^TT-I\|_2\le\delta<1\), then
\(\|T^{-1}\|^2\le(1-\delta)^{-1}\). The \(\lambda_K\), cross, and \(\rho^2\) terms therefore all acquire at most the common factor \((1-\delta)^{-1}\), which is applied at [interval_cells_v2.py:236](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:236>).

One documentation defect: an interval product “contains zero” does not, by itself, prove the exact product equals zero. Thus checks (b) and (d) at [interval_cells_v2.py:171](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:171>) and [interval_cells_v2.py:185](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:185>) are only sanity checks. Exact kernel membership follows instead from the algebraic construction \(Z_1=PZ_f\) and subsequent exact-target Gram–Schmidt at [interval_cells_v2.py:146](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:146>). With that algebraic argument, the cell lemma is valid.

These are formal soundness gaps, even though any resulting error is at binary64-ulp scale and is numerically far below the recorded slack.

### 4. Strict gap and symmetrisation — REFUTED as worded; binary-target version CONFIRMED

[CONFIRMED] `final_gap > 0` is certified for the code’s binary64 `s_target_f`. `tr_exact` and `s_target` are Arb values and the comparison is performed in Arb: [interval_cells_v2.py:90](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:90>). The recorded result is \(0.030405245308261095>0\): [o3_summary.json:12](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/metrics/o3_summary.json:12>).

[REFUTED] `s_target` is not exact \(9\,\operatorname{tr}R/200\). Exact rational reconstruction from the stored diagonal gives

\[
s_{\rm float}-\frac{9\,\operatorname{tr}R}{200}
=\frac{13}{7205759403792793600}
\approx1.8041124150158794\times10^{-18}>0.
\]

Thus the run proves \(B<s_{\rm float}\), where \(s_{\rm float}\) is slightly *larger* than the exact \(s^*\). Because early leaf slacks were not stored, the primary artifacts do not prove that every early leaf also clears the exact target.

This does **not** threaten the separator conclusion itself: the certified `final_gap` proves the binary target is still strictly below \(\operatorname{tr}R/20\). It only leaves the stronger literal \(B\le s^*=0.9\,\operatorname{tr}R/20\) claim unclosed.

[CONFIRMED] The Hermitian part is formed correctly in Arb from exact binary64 components:

\[
\Re H_{ij}=\frac{\Re R_{ij}+\Re R_{ji}}2,\qquad
\Im H_{ij}=\frac{\Im R_{ij}-\Im R_{ji}}2.
\]

See [interval_cells_v2.py:58](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:58>). The raw file is not exactly Hermitian: the code records that fact at [interval_cells_v2.py:38](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:38>).

There is a stale documentation conflict: [STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:73](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:73>) says the float symmetrisation exactly equals the stored matrix, while the later correction at [STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:103](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:103>) and the code say otherwise. The actual certificate is for \(H=(R_{\rm raw}+R_{\rm raw}^H)/2\).

### 5. Covering — UNCHECKED from the listed primary artifacts alone

The mathematical phase reduction is correct: every unit \(x\) can be globally phased so \(x_0\ge0\) is real, and \(y^HRy\) is invariant under the matching phase of \(y\). The pole is covered as discussed in item 1.

The primary coverage JSON reports:

- 3,955,841 leaves;
- extrema reaching binary64 `π` and `2π`;
- total rectangle area equal to the target area.

See [leaves_coverage_2026-10-31.json:2](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/metrics/leaves_coverage_2026-10-31.json:2>) and [leaves_coverage_2026-10-31.json:19](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/metrics/leaves_coverage_2026-10-31.json:19>).

But extrema plus summed area cannot rule out a hole cancelled by an overlap. Therefore this JSON does not independently prove tiling.

The proof document reports a bit-exact reconstruction with 3,955,841 distinct matched leaves and zero uncovered cells at [STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:102](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:102>). The split argument—children sharing the same binary64 midpoint partition their parent—is sound. But the referenced verifier/output and leaf array were not among the enumerated primary artifacts, so the actual bitwise match remains `UNCHECKED` under the requested primary-only rule.

The tiny difference between real \(2\pi\) and binary64 `2*pi` is harmless if L2 is admitted: it is about \(2.45\times10^{-16}\), far below the positive \(10^{-12}\) radius enlargement.

### 6. Journaling — CONFIRMED as aggregate evidence, not per-leaf evidence

The arithmetic is exact:

- Total chunks: \(\lceil3{,}955{,}841/500\rceil=7{,}912\).
- Earlier completed chunks `0..3420`: \(3421\times500=1{,}710{,}500\) leaves.
- Resumed chunks `3421..7911`: \(2{,}245{,}341\) leaves.
- Total: \(1{,}710{,}500+2{,}245{,}341=3{,}955{,}841\).

The driver reads completed chunk IDs and skips them on resume at [o3_run_v2.py:66](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/o3_run_v2.py:66>) and [o3_run_v2.py:131](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/o3_run_v2.py:131>). Therefore `ok_this_run = 2245341` is intentionally resumed-invocation-only, not the total: [o3_summary.json:10](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/metrics/o3_summary.json:10>).

The journal audit reports exact chunk IDs, total \(n\), total `ok`, and total failures:

- 7,912 unique chunks;
- summed `n = ok = 3,955,841`;
- zero failures;
- exact-range coverage true.

See [o3_journal_audit_2026-10-31.json:2](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/metrics/o3_journal_audit_2026-10-31.json:2>) and [o3_journal_audit_2026-10-31.json:7](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/metrics/o3_journal_audit_2026-10-31.json:7>).

- For every chunk: `chunk`, `n`, `ok`, `fails`, and elapsed time; [o3_run_v2.py:166](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/o3_run_v2.py:166>).
