What is not recorded:

- No successful leaf has a per-leaf record.
- No per-leaf `B_up`, \(\kappa\), \(\rho\), \(\delta\), or Arb interval is stored.
- Early chunks `0..3420` have no stored minimum slack or maximum `B_up`.
- Later chunks also have no per-leaf bounds; only the resumed run’s two global extrema.

This limitation is explicitly acknowledged at [o3_journal_audit_2026-10-31.json:19](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/metrics/o3_journal_audit_2026-10-31.json:19>) and [O3_CLOSURE_2026-10-31.md:42](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/O3_CLOSURE_2026-10-31.md:42>).

One additional code point: the driver’s own coverage condition checks only the number of unique IDs, not equality to `range(total_chunks)`: [o3_run_v2.py:190](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/o3_run_v2.py:190>). The separate journal audit’s `cover_complete` result is therefore essential.

### 7. Historical caveats — CONFIRMED not to invalidate v2, with a provenance condition

The earlier memory exception after 3,595,000 leaves does not invalidate O3 v2. That earlier run was not used as the missing tail of the corrected v2 certificate; the proof document says a complete v2 pass was subsequently performed: [STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:81](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:81>) and [STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:103](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/STAGE_C_FINAL_PROOF.md:103>).

The old midpoint rule for \(\lambda_K\) was not rigorous. The current code correctly uses `lam_h.upper()` before clamping against zero at [interval_cells_v2.py:226](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:226>). The current code hashes match the recorded manifest.

Condition: chunk journal records do not contain a code hash per invocation or per chunk. Therefore the assertion that early journal lines were produced by exactly the current corrected code is provenance evidence from the closure document, not something derivable from each journal record.

### 8. Dependencies outside phase4 — CONFIRMED

The running certificate is not phase4-self-contained.

- `s8_admissible_span.py` is imported from the discovery directory at [interval_cells_v2.py:32](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:32>). It supplies both `load_A` and `phi_rows`.
- The instance file is read directly from the submission-package directory at [interval_cells_v2.py:26](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:26>).
- The actual R file used by the certificate is read directly from phase3 at [interval_cells_v2.py:28](<E:/Проверка Гипотез/работаю над проверкой гипотез/Y-17 100 gipotez/experiments/20261028-h-cat56-2-phase4/o3_v2/code/interval_cells_v2.py:28>).
- Runtime correctness also depends on `python-flint`/Arb and NumPy. Importing the external `s8_admissible_span.py` additionally requires SciPy, although its optimizer is not used by O3.

[VERIFIED] The current phase3 R and external instance are byte-identical to the phase4 `o3_v2/inputs` copies whose hashes appear in the journal audit. But the driver hashes only the leaf file during the run; it does not enforce expected hashes for the actual external R, instance, `s8_admissible_span.py`, or code. The external `s8_admissible_span.py` is also absent from the stated O3 code manifest.

## Most likely failure mode and slack

The single strongest concrete failure mode is the **threshold mismatch**: the code checks against a binary64 `s_target` that is \(1.804\times10^{-18}\) above exact \(9\,\operatorname{tr}R/200\), while early per-leaf slacks were discarded. Consequently, the exact-\(s^*\) statement could in principle fail on an early cell whose slack lies between \(0\) and \(1.804\times10^{-18}\), without contradicting any stored journal record.

[INFERRED] The reported \(8.4\times10^{-9}\) minimum is enormous compared with normal 192-bit Arb roundoff: \(2^{-192}\approx1.6\times10^{-58}\) relative, so the scale separation is roughly 49–51 decimal orders. It is also about nine orders larger than binary64-ulp-scale selection errors and the exact-target mismatch. Thus Arb enclosure width is not a plausible numerical threat for the recorded closest cell. Nevertheless, precision alone does not bound width after ill-conditioned inversions, and the primary artifacts do not store the actual internal widths or early minimum slack.

The conditions for a fully clean `PASS` are:

1. Construct the threshold directly in Arb as `tr_exact * 9 / 200`.
2. Keep all maximum, row-sum, and sign decisions in Arb—no `float(...)` selection of Arb endpoints.
3. Re-certify at least chunks `0..3420`, or store chunk-level certified lower slack so the exact-target comparison can be audited.
4. Bind each journal segment to hashes of the executed certificate code, `s8_admissible_span.py`, actual external R, instance, and leaves.
5. Include the bit-exact tiling verifier/output and its leaf input in the primary certificate set.
